===Chinese===

……先生家的客厅里，或任何一户不识生意门道的人家说说，也许有人信；可是任何预审法官，除非是个淌口水的白痴，都无法想象杜·克鲁瓦西耶夫人这样对丈夫百依百顺的女人，此刻会把10万埃居锁在书桌里而对他只字不提；更无法想象一个老公证人，会不趁杜·克鲁瓦西耶先生回城时把这笔存款知会他。”
“老公证人嘛，先生，是到巴黎去了，为了拦住这年轻人的挥霍。”
“我还没有讯问德·埃斯格里尼翁伯爵，”卡缪索开口道，“他的回答会给我指明职责。”
“他押得很严吗？”
“是的。”
“先生，”谢内尔看出前头有险，“这讯问可以照我们的利益办，也可以倒着办。您面前有两条路：或是凭杜·克鲁瓦西耶夫人的证词确立事实——款子在汇票开出之前已存到她处；或是去讯问这桩案子里牵连的不幸青年，他慌乱之中也许什么都记不得，反倒落下把柄。您来裁决哪个更可信：一个不懂生意的女人记错了一笔，还是一个埃斯格里尼翁犯下了伪造罪。”
“这些都文不对题，”卡缪索说，“问题在于：德·埃斯格里尼翁伯爵先生，是否把杜·克鲁瓦西耶写给他的信的下半截，当汇票用了。”
“嗳！他就是用了又怎样！”一个声音突然喊道；卡缪索夫人领着俊俏的生客闯了进来，“就是用了又怎样，谢内尔先生不是已经垫出了兑票的钱——”
她俯身凑近丈夫。
“巴黎助理法官的头一个空缺就是你的；这桩案子里，你是在为国王本人效力；我有凭据；不会忘了你的。”她压低嗓音说给他一个人听。“你眼前这位青年，是德·莫弗里涅斯公爵夫人；你就从来没见过她；放开手脚，替年轻伯爵尽力。”
“诸位，”卡缪索说，“就算预审照着证明年轻伯爵清白的方向去办，法院那边的看法，我担保得了吗？谢内尔先生，还有你，我的心肝，都知道院长先生要的是什么。”
“去去去！”卡缪索夫人说，“你亲自去找米许

===Original===

Michu this morning, and tell him that the Count has been arrested; you will be two against two in that case, I will be bound. *Michu* comes from Paris, and you know he is devoted to the noblesse. Good blood cannot lie.”
At that very moment Mlle. Cadot’s voice was heard in the doorway. She had brought a note, and was waiting for an answer. Camusot went out, and came back again to read the note aloud:

> “M. le Vice-Président begs M. Camusot to sit in audience today and for the next few days, so that there may be a quorum during M. le Président’s absence.”

===Chinese===

……先生，告诉他伯爵被捕了；那样你们就是二对二，我敢担保。米许是巴黎来的，你知道他一心向着贵族。好血统不会撒谎。”
恰在这时，门口传来卡多小姐的声音。她送来一张便条，正候着回音。卡缪索出去了一趟，回来把便条高声念了：

> “副庭长先生恳请卡缪索先生今日及今后数日照常出庭审案，以便院长先生缺席期间凑足法定人数。”

===Original===

“Then there is an end of the preliminary examination!” cried Mme. Camusot. “Did I not tell you, dear, that they would play you some ugly trick? The President has gone off to slander you to the public prosecutor and the President of the Court-Royal. You will be changed before you can make the examination. Is that clear?”
“You will stay, monsieur,” said the Duchess. “The public prosecutor is coming, I hope, in time.”
“When the public prosecutor arrives,” little Mme. Camusot said, with some heat, “he must find all over.﻿—Yes, my dear, yes,” she added, looking full at her amazed husband.﻿—“Ah! old hypocrite of a President, you are setting your wits against us; you shall remember it! You have a mind to help us to a dish of your own making, you shall have two served up to you by your humble servant Cécile Amélie Thirion!﻿—Poor old Blondet! It is lucky for him that the President has taken this journey to turn us out, for now that great oaf of a Joseph Blondet will marry Mlle. Blandureau. I will let Father Blondet have some seeds in return.﻿—As for you, Camusot, go to M. Michu’s, while Mme. la Duchesse and I will go to find old Blondet. You must expect to hear it said all over the town tomorrow that I took a walk with a lover this morning.”
Mme. Camusot took the Duchess’ arm, and they went through the town by deserted streets to avoid any unpleasant adventure on the way to the old Vice-President’s house. Chesnel meanwhile conferred with the young Count in prison; Camusot had arranged a stolen interview. Cook-maids, servants, and the other early risers of a country town, seeing Mme. Camusot and the Duchess taking their way through the back streets, took the young gentleman for an adorer from Paris. That evening, as Cécile Amélie had said, the news of her behavior was circulated about the town, and more than one scandalous rumor was occasioned thereby. Mme. Camusot and her supposed lover found old Blondet in his greenhouse. He greeted his colleague’s wife and her companion, and gave the charming young man a keen, uneasy glance.
“I have the honor to introduce one of my husband’s cousins,” said Mme. Camusot, bringing forward the Duchess; “he is one of the most distinguished horticulturists in Paris; and as he cannot spend more than one day with us, on his way back from Brittany, and has heard of your flowers and plants, I have taken the liberty of coming early.”

===Chinese===

“这么说，预审要被掐断了！”卡缪索夫人喊道，“我不是告诉过你吗，亲爱的，他们会给你使坏？院长跑掉了，去对公诉人和王家法院院长说你的坏话。案子还没来得及审，你就会被撤换掉。这不是明摆着？”
“您留下，先生，”公爵夫人说，“公诉人嘛，我希望来得及。”
“等公诉人到的时候，”小个子卡缪索夫人带着火气说，“得让他看见一切都已经了结。——是啊，亲爱的，是啊，”她补了一句，直勾勾望着她那目瞪口呆的丈夫。——“啊！老伪君子院长，你敢跟我们斗心眼；叫你记得住！你想端你亲手做的菜给我们吃，那我就叫你的卑微的仆人塞西尔·阿梅莉·蒂里翁，回敬你两道！——可怜的老布隆代！院长这趟出门想撵走我们，倒是他的造化：这下子约瑟夫·布隆代那个大憨瓜娶得上布朗迪罗小姐了。回头我送些种子给布隆代老爹作谢礼。——至于你，卡缪索，去米许先生家；我和公爵夫人去找老布隆代。明天全城都会说，我今天早晨陪情人散步去了——你认了吧。”
卡缪索夫人挽起公爵夫人的手臂，两人拣僻静的街道穿城而过，免得去老副庭长家的路上出什么岔子。与此同时，谢内尔到监狱里同年轻伯爵密谈；这次偷偷的会面是卡缪索安排的。厨娘、仆役和外省小城的其他早起者，看见卡缪索夫人同这位青年取道后街，都把这位青年先生当成了巴黎来的追求者。当天晚上，正如塞西尔·阿梅莉所料，她的行踪传遍了全城，惹出了不止一则风言风语。卡缪索夫人和她那位假想情人，在温室里找到了老布隆代。他招呼了同事的夫人和她的同伴，又朝那俊俏青年投去锐利而不安的一瞥。
“我有幸给您引见我丈夫的一位堂弟，”卡缪索夫人说着，把公爵夫人推上前去，“他是巴黎最有名的园艺家之一；他从布列塔尼回程，只能在我们这儿待一天，又久闻您的花草大名，所以我冒昧一早就来了。”

===Original===

“Oh, the gentleman is a horticulturist, is he?” said the old Blondet.
The Duchess bowed.
“This is my coffee-plant,” said Blondet, “and here is a tea-plant.”
“What can have taken M. le Président away from home?” put in Mme. Camusot. “I will wager that his absence concerns M. Camusot.”
“Exactly.﻿—This, monsieur, is the queerest of all cactuses,” he continued, producing a flowerpot which appeared to contain a piece of mildewed rattan; “it comes from Australia. You are very young, sir, to be a horticulturist.”
“Dear M. Blondet, never mind your flowers,” said Mme. Camusot. “*You* are concerned, you and your hopes, and your son’s marriage with Mlle. Blandureau. You are duped by the President.”
“Bah!” said old Blondet, with an incredulous air.
“Yes,” retorted she. “If you cultivated people a little more and your flowers a little less, you would know that the dowry and the hopes you have sown, and watered, and tilled, and weeded are on the point of being gathered now by cunning hands.”
“Madame!﻿—”
“Oh, nobody in the town will have the courage to fly in the President’s face and warn you. I, however, do not belong to the town, and, thanks to this obliging young man, I shall soon be going back to Paris; so I can inform you that Chesnel’s successor has made formal proposals for Mlle. Claire Blandureau’s hand on behalf of young du Ronceret, who is to have fifty thousand crowns from his parents. As for Fabien, he has made up his mind to receive a call to the bar, so as to gain an appointment as judge.”
Old Blondet dropped the flowerpot which he had brought out for the Duchess to see.
“Oh, my cactus! Oh, my son! and Mlle. Blandureau!﻿ ﻿… Look here! the cactus flower is broken to pieces.”
“No,” Mme. Camusot answered, laughing; “everything can be put right. If you have a mind to see your son a judge in another month, we will tell you how you must set to work﻿—”
“Step this way, sir, and you will see my pelargoniums, an enchanting sight while they are in flower﻿—” Then he added to Mme. Camusot, “Why did you speak of these matters while your cousin was present.”
“All depends upon him,” riposted Mme. Camusot. “Your son’s appointment is lost forever if you let fall a word about this young man.”
“Bah!”
“The young man is a flower﻿—”
“Ah!”

===Chinese===

“哦，这位先生是园艺家，是吗？”老布隆代说。
公爵夫人欠了欠身。
“这是我的咖啡树，”布隆代说，“这棵是茶树。”
“院长先生能有什么事出了门呢？”卡缪索夫人插嘴道，“我敢打赌，他这一走同卡缪索先生有关。”
“正是。——先生，这一棵是全部仙人掌里最古怪的，”他接着说，端出一只花盆，里面像是栽着一截发了霉的藤条，“它来自澳洲。先生，您这么年轻，就当园艺家了。”
“亲爱的布隆代先生，别管您的花了，”卡缪索夫人说，“要紧的是您，您的指望，您儿子同布朗迪罗小姐的婚事。您被院长骗了。”
“嗐！”老布隆代一脸不信。
“真的。”她反驳道，“您若少伺候点花、多伺候点人，就会知道：您播种、浇水、耕耘、除草养出来的那份嫁妆和指望，眼看就要被一双狡猾的手收割去了。”
“夫人！——”
“唉，城里没有一个人敢当面顶撞院长、给您提个醒。我呢，不算这城里的人，况且托这位热心青年的福，我很快就要回巴黎去了；所以我才敢告诉您：谢内尔的继任者已经替小迪·龙瑟雷正式向克莱尔·布朗迪罗小姐求婚——他从父母那里可得5万埃居。至于法比安，他已打定主意取得律师资格，好谋一个法官的位子。”
老布隆代手里那盆端出来给公爵夫人看的仙人掌，掉在了地上。
“哦，我的仙人掌！哦，我的儿子！还有布朗迪罗小姐！……瞧啊！仙人掌的花摔得粉碎。”
“不，”卡缪索夫人笑着回答，“样样都能补救。您若想一个月后看见儿子当上法官，我们就告诉您该怎么办——”

===Original===

“He is the Duchesse de Maufrigneuse, sent here by His Majesty to save young d’Esgrignon, whom they arrested yesterday on a charge of forgery brought against him by du Croisier. Mme. la Duchesse has authority from the Keeper of the Seals; he will ratify any promises that she makes to us﻿—”
“My cactus is all right!” exclaimed Blondet, peering at his precious plant.﻿—“Go on, I am listening.”
“Take counsel with Camusot and Michu to hush up the affair as soon as possible, and your son will get the appointment. It will come in time enough to baffle du Ronceret’s underhand dealings with the Blandureaus. Your son will be something better than assistant judge; he will have M. Camusot’s post within the year. The public prosecutor will be here today. M. Sauvager will be obliged to resign, I expect, after his conduct in this affair. At the court my husband will show you documents which completely exonerate the Count and prove that the forgery was a trap of du Croisier’s own setting.”
Old Blondet went into the Olympic circus where his six thousand pelargoniums stood, and made his bow to the Duchess.
“Monsieur,” said he, “if your wishes do not exceed the law, this thing may be done.”
“Monsieur,” returned the Duchess, “send in your resignation to M. Chesnel tomorrow, and I will promise you that your son shall be appointed within the week; but you must not resign until you have had confirmation of my promise from the public prosecutor. You men of law will come to a better understanding among yourselves. Only let him know that the Duchesse de Maufrigneuse had pledged her word to you. And not a word as to my journey hither,” she added.
The old judge kissed her hand and began recklessly to gather his best flowers for her.
“Can you think of it? Give them to madame,” said the Duchess. “A young man should not have flowers about him when he has a pretty woman on his arm.”
“Before you go down to the court,” added Mme. Camusot, “ask Chesnel’s successor about those proposals that he made in the name of M. and Mme. du Ronceret.”
Old Blondet, quite overcome by this revelation of the President’s duplicity, stood planted on his feet by the wicket gate, looking after the two women as they hurried away through by-streets home again. The edifice raised so painfully during ten years for his beloved son was crumbling visibly before his eyes. Was it possible?

===Chinese===

“这边走，先生，请看我的天竺葵，正当花期，美不胜收——”随即他又对卡缪索夫人说：“这些事，您怎么当着您堂弟的面讲。”
“全看他了。”卡缪索夫人回敬道，“这青年的事，您若走漏半个字，您儿子的任命就永远完了。”
“嗐！”
“这青年本人就是一朵花——”
“啊！”
“他就是德·莫弗里涅斯公爵夫人，奉陛下之命来救小德·埃斯格里尼翁——昨天他们凭杜·克鲁瓦西耶的伪造控告把他逮捕了。公爵夫人有掌玺大臣的授权；她对我们许下的任何承诺，掌玺大臣都会追认——”
“我的仙人掌没事！”布隆代端详着那盆宝贝，喊道。——“接着说，我听着呢。”
“同卡缪索和米许商量着，尽快把事情压下去，您儿子就能拿到任命。而且来得正好，能搅了迪·龙瑟雷同布朗迪罗家的暗盘。您儿子到手的还不止助理法官；不出一年，他会坐上卡缪索先生的位子。公诉人今天就到。索瓦热先生在这桩案子里的行径之后，我看只好辞职了。在法院，我丈夫会拿文书给您看，那些文书彻底开脱了伯爵，证明所谓伪造不过是杜·克鲁瓦西耶亲手设下的圈套。”
老布隆代走进他那座奥林匹亚竞技场——6000盆天竺葵的所在——向公爵夫人深深一躬。
“先生，”他说，“只要您的愿望不逾法律，此事可办。”
“先生，”公爵夫人答道，“明天把辞呈交给谢内尔先生，我答应您：一星期之内，您儿子走马上任；但在公诉人向我这个承诺作证之前，先不要辞。你们法律人之间自会商量得更妥帖。只要让他知道，德·莫弗里涅斯公爵夫人对您许下了诺言。还有，我来此一趟的事，一个字也不许说。”她补了一句。
老法官吻了吻她的手，开始不管不顾地为她采他最好的花。
“您想的什么呀？把花送给夫人吧，”公爵夫人说，“一个青年，臂弯里挽着漂亮女人，身边不该带花。”
“下法院之前，”卡缪索夫人添了一句，“去问问谢内尔的继任者，他替迪·龙瑟雷先生和夫人提亲那件事。”
老布隆代被院长两面三刀的这一揭露击垮了，钉在小边门旁，望着两位夫人急匆匆穿小街回去了。十年来他为爱子一砖一瓦苦心垒起的殿堂，正在他眼前一寸寸塌落。这怎么可能？

===Original===

He suspected some trick, and hurried away to Chesnel’s successor.
At half-past nine, before the court was sitting, Vice-President Blondet, Camusot, and Michu met with remarkable punctuality in the council chamber. Blondet locked the door with some precautions when Camusot and Michu came in together.
“Well, Mr. Vice-President,” began Michu, “M. Sauvager, without consulting the public prosecutor, has issued a warrant for the apprehension of one Comte d’Esgrignon, in order to serve a grudge borne against him by one du Croisier, an enemy of the King’s government. It is a regular topsy-turvy affair. The President, for his part, goes away, and thereby puts a stop to the preliminary examination! And we know nothing of the matter. Do they, by any chance, mean to force our hand?”
“This is the first word I have heard of it,” said the Vice-President. He was furious with the President for stealing a march on him with the Blandureaus. Chesnel’s successor, the du Roncerets’ man, had just fallen into a snare set by the old judge; the truth was out, he knew the secret.
“It is lucky that we spoke to you about the matter, my dear master,” said Camusot, “or you might have given up all hope of seating your son on the bench or of marrying him to Mlle. Blandureau.”
“But it is no question of my son, nor of his marriage,” said the Vice-President; “we are talking of young Comte d’Esgrignon. Is he or is he not guilty?”
“It seems that Chesnel deposited the amount to meet the bill with Mme. du Croisier,” said Michu, “and a crime has been made of a mere irregularity. According to the charge, the Count made use of the lower half of a letter bearing du Croisier’s signature as a draft which he cashed at the Kellers’.”
“An imprudent thing to do,” was Camusot’s comment.
“But why is du Croisier proceeding against him if the amount was paid in beforehand?” asked Vice-President Blondet.
“He does not know that the money was deposited with his wife; or he pretends that he does not know,” said Camusot.
“It is a piece of provincial spite,” said Michu.
“Still it looks like a forgery to me,” said old Blondet. No passion could obscure judicial clear-sightedness in him.
“Do you think so?” returned Camusot.

===Chinese===

他疑心里面有诈，急忙找谢内尔的继任者去了。
九点半，开庭之前，副庭长布隆代、卡缪索和米许在评议室里准时得罕见地碰了头。卡缪索和米许一同进来后，布隆代小心地锁上了门。
“怎么样，副庭长先生，”米许先开口，“索瓦热先生不请示公诉人，就签发了拘捕一位德·埃斯格里尼翁伯爵的拘票，为的是替一个杜·克鲁瓦西耶——国王政府之敌——出一口怨气。整个一个乾坤颠倒。院长呢，一走了之，顺势把预审掐断了！我们对此竟一无所知。莫非他们想逼我们就范？”
“这话我还是头一回听见。”副庭长说。他正为院长在布朗迪罗家抢了先而怒火中烧。谢内尔的继任者，迪·龙瑟雷家的人，刚落进老法官设下的圈套；真相大白，秘密他已知道。
“幸亏我们把这件事对您说了，我亲爱的老师，”卡缪索说，“不然您把儿子送上审判席、同布朗迪罗小姐结亲的指望，就全完了。”
“现在谈的不是我儿子，也不是他的婚事，”副庭长说，“我们谈的是小德·埃斯格里尼翁伯爵。他有罪还是没罪？”
“据说是谢内尔把兑票的款子存到了杜·克鲁瓦西耶夫人手里，”米许说，“一桩不合手续的事，被做成了罪。照控方的说法，伯爵把杜·克鲁瓦西耶署名的信的下半截当汇票，到凯勒兄弟那里兑了钱。”
“办得轻率。”卡缪索评了一句。
“可款子既然预先付了，杜·克鲁瓦西耶为什么还要告他？”副庭长布隆代问。
“他不知道钱存在他妻子那里；要么就是装不知道。”卡缪索说。
“这是外省式的挟私报复。”米许说。
“可我看着还是像伪造。”老布隆代说。任何激情都遮不住他司法上的清明。
“您这样看？”卡缪索应道。

===Original===

“But, at the outset, supposing that the Count had no business to draw upon du Croisier, there would still be no forgery of the signature; and the Count believed that he had a right to draw on Croisier when Chesnel advised him that the money had been placed to his credit.”
“Well, then, where is the forgery?” asked Blondet. “It is the intent to defraud which constitutes forgery in a civil action.”
“Oh, it is clear, if you take du Croisier’s version for truth, that the signature was diverted from its purpose to obtain a sum of money in spite of du Croisier’s contrary injunction to his bankers,” Camusot answered.
“Gentlemen,” said Blondet, “this seems to me to be a mere trifle, a quibble.﻿—Suppose you had the money, I ought perhaps to have waited until I had your authorization; but I, Comte d’Esgrignon, was pressed for money, so I﻿—Come, come, your prosecution is a piece of revengeful spite. Forgery is defined by the law as an attempt to obtain any advantage which rightfully belongs to another. There is no forgery here, according to the letter of the Roman law, nor according to the spirit of modern jurisprudence (always from the point of a civil action, for we are not here concerned with the falsification of public or authentic documents). Between private individuals the essence of a forgery is the intent to defraud; where is it in this case? In what times are we living, gentlemen? Here is the President going away to balk a preliminary examination which ought to be over by this time! Until today I did not know M. le Président, but he shall have the benefit of arrears; from this time forth he shall draft his decisions himself. You must set about this affair with all possible speed, M. Camusot.”
“Yes,” said Michu. “In my opinion, instead of letting the young man out on bail, we ought to pull him out of this mess at once. Everything turns on the examination of du Croisier and his wife. You might summons them to appear while the court is sitting, M. Camusot; take down their depositions before four o’clock, send in your report tonight, and we will give our decision in the morning before the court sits.”
“We will settle what course to pursue while the barristers are pleading,” said Vice-President Blondet, addressing Camusot.
And with that the three judges put on their robes and went into court.
At noon Mlle.

“可是，首先，就算伯爵无权向杜·克鲁瓦西耶支款，签名也谈不上伪造；何况谢内尔通知伯爵款子已记入他名下时，伯爵相信自己有权支取。”
“那么，伪造在哪里？”布隆代问，“民事案件里，构成伪造的是欺诈的意图。”
“哦，若照杜·克鲁瓦西耶的说法为准，事情就清楚：签名被挪作他用，在杜·克鲁瓦西耶已明令其银行拒付之后，仍骗取了一笔钱。”卡缪索答道。
“诸位，”布隆代说，“依我看这不过是一桩小事，一句狡辩。——就算钱在您手里，我或许该先得到您的授权；可我，德·埃斯格里尼翁伯爵，等钱用，所以我就——得了，得了，你们的这场公诉，是挟私报复。法律给伪造下的定义，是图谋获取本属他人的利益。这里没有伪造，按罗马法的字面没有，按现代法学的精神也没有（始终就民事诉讼而言，因为我们此处不涉及公文或公证文书的造假）。私人之间，伪造的要点在欺诈的意图；本案的意图在哪里？诸位，我们活的是什么年头？院长居然一走了之，来阻挠一场此刻本该结束的预审！到今天我才算认识了院长先生，可他旧账新账一起算；从今往后，他的裁决书请他自己起草。这桩案子，卡缪索先生，您要全速去办。”
“正是，”米许说，“依我的意思，与其放这年轻人保释外出，不如干脆把他从泥坑里拽出来。一切系于对杜·克鲁瓦西耶夫妇的讯问。开庭期间把他们传到，卡缪索先生；四点钟之前录完口供，今晚把案卷报上来，明晨开庭之前，我们作出裁定。”
“律师们辩护的时候，我们把步骤定下来。”副庭长布隆代对卡缪索说。
说罢，三位法官披上法袍，进了法庭。
正午时分，阿尔芒德小姐……

===Original===

Armande and the Bishop reached the Hôtel d’Esgrignon; Chesnel and M. Couturier were there to meet them. There was a sufficiently short conference between the prelate and Mme. du Croisier’s director, and the latter set out at once to visit his charge.
At eleven o’clock that morning du Croisier received a summons to appear in the examining magistrate’s office between one and two in the afternoon. Thither he betook himself, consumed by well-founded suspicions. It was impossible that the President should have foreseen the arrival of the Duchesse de Maufrigneuse upon the scene, the return of the public prosecutor, and the hasty confabulation of his learned brethren; so he had omitted to trace out a plan for du Croisier’s guidance in the event of the preliminary examination taking place. Neither of the pair imagined that the proceedings would be hurried on in this way. Du Croisier obeyed the summons at once; he wanted to know how M. Camusot was disposed to act. So he was compelled to answer the questions put to him. Camusot addressed him in summary fashion with the six following inquiries:﻿—
“Was the signature on the bill alleged to be a forgery in your handwriting?﻿—Had you previously done business with M. le Comte d’Esgrignon?﻿—Was not M. le Comte d’Esgrignon in the habit of drawing upon you, with or without advice?﻿—Did you not write a letter authorizing M. d’Esgrignon to rely upon you at any time?﻿—Had not Chesnel squared the account not once, but many times already?﻿—Were you not away from home when this took place?”
All these questions the banker answered in the affirmative. In spite of wordy explanations, the magistrate always brought him back to a “Yes” or “No.” When the questions and answers alike had been resumed in the procès-verbal, the examining magistrate brought out a final thunderbolt.
“Was du Croisier aware that the money destined to meet the bill had been deposited with him, du Croisier, according to Chesnel’s declaration, and a letter of advice sent by the said Chesnel to the Comte d’Esgrignon, five days before the date of the bill?”
That last question frightened du Croisier. He asked what was meant by it, and whether he was supposed to be the defendant and M. le Comte d’Esgrignon the plaintiff? He called the magistrate’s attention to the fact that if the money had been deposited with him, there was no ground for the action.

===Chinese===

……同主教大人到了埃斯格里尼翁府；谢内尔和库蒂里耶先生已在那里迎候。主教大人同杜·克鲁瓦西耶夫人的神师作了一次相当简短的商谈，后者随即动身去探望他管领的灵魂。
当天上午11点，杜·克鲁瓦西耶接到传票，命他下午1点到2点之间到预审法官办公室应讯。他怀着事出有因的疑虑前往。院长不可能预见到德·莫弗里涅斯公爵夫人驾到、公诉人回任、以及他那些博学同僚的紧急磋商；所以他没有为杜·克鲁瓦西耶拟出一份预审一旦进行时的应讯方略。这一对谁也没想到程序会推进得这样快。杜·克鲁瓦西耶立刻应讯；他正想知道卡缪索先生打算怎么办。于是他不得不回答向他提出的问题。卡缪索以撮要的方式向他提了以下六个问题：——
“那张被指为伪造的汇票上的签名，是否出自您的手笔？——您以前同德·埃斯格里尼翁伯爵先生有过银钱往来吗？——德·埃斯格里尼翁伯爵先生不是常有支取您的习惯吗，通知与否不拘？——您是否写过一封信，授权德·埃斯格里尼翁先生随时凭恃于您？——谢内尔不是结清过账目，而且不止一次，是许多次了吗？——此事发生时，您是否不在家中？”
这些问题，银行家一概作了肯定回答。任凭他解释千言万语，法官总把他拉回到一个“是”或“否”。等问答都录进了笔录，预审法官抛出了最后一记炸雷。
“杜·克鲁瓦西耶是否知道：据谢内尔的声明，兑付该汇票的款项，已经存到了他杜·克鲁瓦西耶处，并有上述谢内尔致德·埃斯格里尼翁伯爵的通知信一封，日期在汇票之前五天？”
最后这个问题吓着了杜·克鲁瓦西耶。他问这话是什么意思，是不是把他当成了被告，反倒把德·埃斯格里尼翁伯爵先生当原告？他提请法官注意：倘若款项已存到他处，诉讼便没有根据。

===Original===

“Justice is seeking information,” said the magistrate, as he dismissed the witness, but not before he had taken down du Croisier’s last observation.
“But the money, sir﻿—”
“The money is at your house.”
Chesnel, likewise summoned, came forward to explain the matter. The truth of his assertions was borne out by Mme. du Croisier’s deposition. The Count had already been examined. Prompted by Chesnel, he produced du Croisier’s first letter, in which he begged the Count to draw upon him without the insulting formality of depositing the amount beforehand. The Comte d’Esgrignon next brought out a letter in Chesnel’s handwriting, by which the notary advised him of the deposit of a hundred thousand crowns with M. du Croisier. With such primary facts as these to bring forward as evidence, the young Count’s innocence was bound to emerge triumphantly from a court of law.
Du Croisier went home from the court, his face white with rage, and the foam of repressed fury on his lips. His wife was sitting by the fireside in the drawing-room at work upon a pair of slippers for him. She trembled when she looked into his face, but her mind was made up.
“Madame,” he stammered out, “what deposition is this that you made before the magistrate? You have dishonored, ruined, and betrayed me!”
“I have saved you, monsieur,” answered she. “If some day you will have the honor of connecting yourself with the d’Esgrignons by marrying your niece to the Count, it will be entirely owing to my conduct today.”
“A miracle!” cried he. “Balaam’s ass has spoken. Nothing will astonish me after this. And where are the hundred thousand crowns which (so M. Camusot tells me) are here in my house?”
“Here they are,” said she, pulling out a bundle of banknotes from beneath the cushions of her settee. “I have not committed mortal sin by declaring that M. Chesnel gave them into my keeping.”
“While I was away?”
“You were not here.”
“Will you swear that to me on your salvation?”
“I swear it,” she said composedly.
“Then why did you say nothing to me about it?” demanded he.
“I was wrong there,” said his wife, “but my mistake was all for your good. Your niece will be Marquise d’Esgrignon some of these days, and you will perhaps be a deputy, if you behave well in this deplorable business. You have gone too far; you must find out how to get back again.”

“司法是在了解情况。”法官说着，打发证人退出；不过退出之前，杜·克鲁瓦西耶最后那句话也照录在案。
“可是那笔钱，先生——”
“钱就在您府上。”
谢内尔也接到传唤，上前来把事情说清。他的陈述，有杜·克鲁瓦西耶夫人的证词作证。伯爵也已受讯。在谢内尔提示下，他出示了杜·克鲁瓦西耶最早的那封信，信中恳请伯爵尽管支取，不必先存票款、受那侮辱性的俗套。接着，德·埃斯格里尼翁伯爵又拿出谢内尔亲笔的一封信，通知他10万埃居已存到杜·克鲁瓦西耶先生处。有这些基本事实摆上法庭作证，年轻伯爵的清白，注定会堂堂正正地从法院凯旋。
杜·克鲁瓦西耶从法院回家，脸气得发白，强压的怒火在唇边起了沫。他妻子正坐在客厅炉边，给他缝一双拖鞋。她一看他的脸就发抖，可她主意已定。
“夫人，”他结结巴巴地说，“您在法官面前作的是什么证？您毁了我的名誉，毁了我的家业，还出卖了我！”
“我是救了您，先生。”她答道，“有朝一日，您若因内侄女嫁伯爵而荣幸地同埃斯格里尼翁家结亲，那全亏我今天的作为。”
“真是奇迹！”他喊道，“巴兰的驴子开口说话了。此后再没有什么能叫我吃惊了。那么，照卡缪索先生的说法，在我家的那10万埃居在哪里？”
“在这儿。”她说着，从长椅坐垫底下抽出一捆钞票。“我声明是谢内尔先生交我保管的，这不算犯死罪。”
“趁我不在家？”
“您不在家。”
“您敢拿您的灵魂得救向我起誓？”
“我起誓。”她平静地说。
“那您先前为什么对我只字不提？”他逼问。
“这上头是我不对，”他妻子说，“可我的错处全为着您好。您的内侄女不日就是埃斯格里尼翁侯爵夫人；只要您在这桩倒霉案子里表现得好，您说不定还能当上议员。您走过头了；您得想法子收回来。”

===Original===

Du Croisier, under stress of painful agitation, strode up and down his drawing-room; while his wife, in no less agitation, awaited the result of this exercise. Du Croisier at length rang the bell.
“I am not at home to anyone tonight,” he said, when the man appeared; “shut the gates; and if anyone calls, tell them that your mistress and I have gone into the country. We shall start directly after dinner, and dinner must be half an hour earlier than usual.”
---
The great news was discussed that evening in every drawing-room; little shopkeepers, working folk, beggars, the noblesse, the merchant class﻿—the whole town, in short, was talking of the Comte d’Esgrignon’s arrest on a charge of forgery. The Comte d’Esgrignon would be tried in the Assize Court; he would be condemned and branded. Most of those who cared for the honor of the family denied the fact. At nightfall Chesnel went to Mme. Camusot and escorted the stranger to the Hôtel d’Esgrignon. Poor Mlle. Armande was expecting him; she led the fair Duchess to her own room, which she had given up to her, for his lordship the Bishop occupied Victurnien’s chamber; and, left alone with her guest, the noble woman glanced at the Duchess with most piteous eyes.
“You owed help, indeed, madame, to the poor boy who ruined himself for your sake,” she said, “the boy to whom we are all of us sacrificing ourselves.”
The Duchess had already made a woman’s survey of Mlle. d’Esgrignon’s room; the cold, bare, comfortless chamber, that might have been a nun’s cell, was like a picture of the life of the heroic woman before her. The Duchess saw it all﻿—past, present, and future﻿—with rising emotion, felt the incongruity of her presence, and could not keep back the falling tears that made answer for her.
But in Mlle. Armande the Christian overcame Victurnien’s aunt. “Ah, I was wrong; forgive me, Mme. la Duchesse; you did not know how poor we were, and my nephew was incapable of the admission. And besides, now that I see you, I can understand all﻿—even the crime!”
And Mlle. Armande, withered and thin and white, but beautiful as those tall austere slender figures which German art alone can paint, had tears too in her eyes.
“Do not fear, dear angel,” the Duchess said at last; “he is safe.”
“Yes, but honor?﻿—and his career? Chesnel told me; the King knows the truth.”
“We will think of a way of repairing the evil,” said the Duchess.

===Chinese===

杜·克鲁瓦西耶在痛苦的激动中，在客厅里大步踱来踱去；他的妻子，激动不亚于他，等着这场踱步的结果。杜·克鲁瓦西耶终于按了铃。
“今晚我谁也不见，”仆人来了，他说，“关上大门；有人来，就说主母和我下乡去了。我们饭后立刻动身，晚饭比平时提前半个钟头。”
---
当天晚上，这个大新闻在每一间客厅里被议论；小店主、做工的、乞丐、贵族、商贾——总之全城都在谈德·埃斯格里尼翁伯爵因伪造罪被捕的事。德·埃斯格里尼翁伯爵要上重罪法院受审；他要被判罪、烙印。关心这家体面的人，大多矢口否认。傍晚，谢内尔到卡缪索夫人家，把那位生客送到埃斯格里尼翁府。可怜的阿尔芒德小姐正等着她；她把美丽的公爵夫人领进自己的房间——那间房已让给了她，因为主教大人占着维克图尼安的屋子；同客人单独相对时，这位高贵的妇人用最凄楚的眼神望着公爵夫人。
“您对那个为了您的缘故毁了自己的可怜孩子，的确欠着一份援手，”她说，“我们大家都在为他牺牲自己。”
公爵夫人已用女人的眼光把德·埃斯格里尼翁小姐的房间扫视了一遍；那间清冷、空疏、没有一点安逸的屋子，本可以做修女的静室，恰是眼前这位崇高妇人一生的写照。公爵夫人把这一切——过去、现在和将来——都看在眼里，情绪汹涌起来，感到自己置身此地多么不称，止不住落下的泪水替她作了回答。
可是在阿尔芒德小姐身上，基督徒压倒了维克图尼安的姑妈。“啊，是我错了；原谅我，公爵夫人；您不知道我们有多穷，而我侄儿又说不出口。再说，如今见了您，我全明白了——连那桩罪也明白了！”
阿尔芒德小姐形容枯槁、瘦削、苍白，却依然美丽，像唯有德国艺术才能画出的那种修长、严峻、颀长的身影；她眼里也噙着泪。
“别怕，亲爱的天使，”公爵夫人终于说，“他安全了。”
“是，可名誉呢？——还有他的前程？谢内尔告诉我了；国王知道真相。”
“我们会想出补救的办法。”公爵夫人说。

===Original===

Mlle. Armande went downstairs to the salon, and found the Collection of Antiquities complete to a man. Every one of them had come, partly to do honor to the Bishop, partly to rally round the Marquis; but Chesnel, posted in the antechamber, warned each new arrival to say no word of the affair, that the aged Marquis might never know that such a thing had been. The loyal Frank was quite capable of killing his son or du Croisier; for either the one or the other must have been guilty of death in his eyes. It chanced, strangely enough, that he talked more of Victurnien than usual; he was glad that his son had gone back to Paris. The King would give Victurnien a place before very long; the King was interesting himself at last in the d’Esgrignons. And his friends, their hearts dead within them, praised Victurnien’s conduct to the skies. Mlle. Armande prepared the way for her nephew’s sudden appearance among them by remarking to her brother that Victurnien would be sure to come to see them, and that he must be even then on his way.
“Bah!” said the Marquis, standing with his back to the hearth, “if he is doing well where he is, he ought to stay there, and not be thinking of the joy it would give his old father to see him again. The King’s service has the first claim.”
Scarcely one of those present heard the words without a shudder. Justice might give over a d’Esgrignon to the executioner’s branding iron. There was a dreadful pause. The old Marquise de Castéran could not keep back a tear that stole down over her rouge, and turned her head away to hide it.
Next day at noon, in the sunny weather, a whole excited population was dispersed in groups along the high street, which ran through the heart of the town, and nothing was talked of but the great affair. Was the Count in prison or was he not?﻿—All at once the Comte d’Esgrignon’s well-known tilbury was seen driving down the Rue Saint-Blaise; it had evidently come from the Prefecture, the Count himself was on the box seat, and by his side sat a charming young man, whom nobody recognized. The pair were laughing and talking and in great spirits. They wore Bengal roses in their buttonholes. Altogether, it was a theatrical surprise which words fail to describe.

===Chinese===

阿尔芒德小姐下楼来到客厅，发现古物陈列室的人一个不落全到了。人人都是为敬主教而来，也为围在侯爵身边聚拢而来；但谢内尔守在前厅，叮嘱每一位新到的客人对此事只字不提，好让年迈的侯爵永远不知道有过这样一桩事。这个忠心的法兰克人，是完全干得出杀死儿子或杀死杜·克鲁瓦西耶的；因为在他眼里，二者必有其一是死罪。说来也怪，这天他谈起维克图尼安比平日更多；他很高兴儿子回了巴黎。国王不久就会给维克图尼安一个职位；国王终于想起埃斯格里尼翁家了。而他的朋友们，心都已死在腔子里，却把维克图尼安的行止夸上了天。阿尔芒德小姐对哥哥说，维克图尼安准会来看他们，说不定此刻已在路上——为侄儿在他们中间的突然露面预先铺路。
“嗐！”侯爵背壁炉站着，说，“他在那边干得好，就该待在那边，不必惦念让老父亲再见他一面的欢喜。国王的差使是头一位的。”
满座之人，听这话不打寒战的几乎没有。司法也许会把一个埃斯格里尼翁交给刽子手的烙铁。一阵可怕的沉默。老德·卡斯特朗侯爵夫人拦不住一滴泪，泪珠悄悄滚过胭脂，她扭过头去遮掩。
第二天正午，晴朗的天气里，激动的人群三五成堆，散在贯穿全城的大街上，谈的只有那桩大案。伯爵到底在不在押？——突然，德·埃斯格里尼翁伯爵那辆人人认得的双轮轻马车，驶下了圣布拉兹街；显然是从省长署那边来的，伯爵本人坐在车夫座上，身边是一位谁也不认识的俊俏青年。两人又说又笑，兴高采烈，钮扣眼里都插着孟加拉玫瑰。总之，那是一场言语无法形容的戏剧性的意外。

===Original===

At ten o’clock the court had decided to dismiss the charge, stating their very sufficient reasons for setting the Count at liberty, in a document which contained a thunderbolt for du Croisier, in the shape of an *inasmuch* that gave the Count the right to institute proceedings for libel. Old Chesnel was walking up the Grand Rue, as if by accident, telling all who cared to hear him that du Croisier had set the most shameful of snares for the d’Esgrignons’ honor, and that it was entirely owing to the forbearance and magnanimity of the family that he was not prosecuted for slander.
On the evening of that famous day, after the Marquis d’Esgrignon had gone to bed, the Count, Mlle. Armande, and the Chevalier were left with the handsome young page, now about to return to Paris. The charming cavalier’s sex could not be hidden from the Chevalier, and he alone, besides the three officials and Mme. Camusot, knew that the Duchess had been among them.
“The house is saved,” began Chesnel, “but after this shock it will take a hundred years to rise again. The debts must be paid now; you must marry an heiress, M. le Comte, there is nothing left for you to do.”
“And take her where you may find her,” said the Duchess.
“A second mésalliance!” exclaimed Mlle. Armande.
The Duchess began to laugh.
“It is better to marry than to die,” she said. As she spoke she drew from her waistcoat pocket a tiny crystal phial that came from the court apothecary.
Mlle. Armande shrank away in horror. Old Chesnel took the fair Maufrigneuse’s hand, and kissed it without permission.
“Are you all out of your minds here?” continued the Duchess. “Do you really expect to live in the fifteenth century when the rest of the world has reached the nineteenth? My dear children, there is no noblesse nowadays; there is no aristocracy left! Napoleon’s Code Civil made an end of the parchments, exactly as cannon made an end of feudal castles. When you have some money, you will be very much more of nobles than you are now. Marry anybody you please, Victurnien, you will raise your wife to your rank; that is the most substantial privilege left to the French noblesse. Did not M. de Talleyrand marry Mme. Grandt without compromising his position? Remember that Louis XIV took the Widow Scarron for his wife.”
“He did not marry her for her money,” interposed Mlle. Armande.

在那个著名的日子的晚上，德·埃斯格里尼翁侯爵就寝之后，客厅里剩下伯爵、阿尔芒德小姐和骑士先生，陪着那位即将返回巴黎的俊俏青年侍从。这位可爱骑士的真实性别，瞒不过骑士先生；除了那三位司法官和卡缪索夫人，只有他知道公爵夫人来过此地。
“门第是保住了，”谢内尔先开口，“可经此一震，要一百年才缓得过来。债如今都得还；您必须娶一位继承女，伯爵先生，您没有别的路。”
“而且到哪里去找就娶哪里的。”公爵夫人说。
“又是一桩门不当户不对！”阿尔芒德小姐喊道。
公爵夫人笑起来。
“结婚总比死强。”她说。说话间，她从背心口袋里摸出一只小小的水晶瓶，得自法院的药剂师。
阿尔芒德小姐惊骇地缩了一下。老谢内尔抓起美丽的莫弗里涅斯的手，没经允许就吻了上去。
“你们这家人都疯了不成？”公爵夫人接着说，“全世界都到了十九世纪，你们真打算活在十五世纪里？我亲爱的孩子们，如今没有什么贵族；贵族根本不存在了！拿破仑的民法典废掉了羊皮文书，正如大炮废掉了封建城堡。等你们有了钱，你们会比现在更像贵族一百倍。爱娶谁就娶谁，维克图尼安，你会把妻子提到你的等级；这是法兰西贵族剩下的最实惠的一项特权。德·塔列朗先生娶格朗夫人，不是没有损害他的地位吗？别忘了路易十四娶的是斯卡龙寡妇。”
“他娶她可不是图她的钱。”阿尔芒德小姐插了一句。

“If the Comtesse d’Esgrignon were one du Croisier’s niece, for instance, would you receive her?” asked Chesnel.
“Perhaps,” replied the Duchess; “but the King, beyond all doubt, would be very glad to see her.﻿—So you do not know what is going on in the world?” continued she, seeing the amazement in their faces. “Victurnien has been in Paris; he knows how things go there. We had more influence under Napoleon. Marry Mlle. Duval, Victurnien; she will be just as much Marquise d’Esgrignon as I am Duchesse de Maufrigneuse.”
“All is lost﻿—even honor!” said the Chevalier, with a wave of the hand.
“Goodbye, Victurnien,” said the Duchess, kissing her lover on the forehead; “we shall not see each other again. Live on your lands; that is the best thing for you to do; the air of Paris is not at all good for you.”
“Diane!” the young Count cried despairingly.
“Monsieur, you forget yourself strangely,” the Duchess retorted coolly, as she laid aside her role of man and mistress, and became not merely an angel again, but a duchess, and not only a duchess, but Molière’s Célimène.
The Duchesse de Maufrigneuse made a stately bow to these four personages, and drew from the Chevalier his last tear of admiration at the service of *le beau sexe*.
“How like she is to the Princess Goritza!” he exclaimed in a low voice.
Diane had disappeared. The crack of the postilion’s whip told Victurnien that the fair romance of his first love was over. While peril lasted, Diane could still see her lover in the young Count; but out of danger, she despised him for the weakling that he was.

Joseph Blondet sat in his father’s seat at the court till the end of his days; there was not the faintest chance of promotion for him, but he became Mlle. Blandereau’s husband; and she, no doubt, is leading today, in the little flower-covered brick house, as dull a life as any carp in a marble basin. “也许，”公爵夫人答道，“可国王呢，毫无疑问，会乐意见她的。——这么说，你们不知道外面世界变成什么样了？”她看他们脸上的惊愕，接着说，“维克图尼安在巴黎待过；他知道那边行事的路数。拿破仑时代我们影响还更大些。娶迪瓦尔小姐吧，维克图尼安；她做埃斯格里尼翁侯爵夫人，同我做莫弗里涅斯公爵夫人，分量一模一样。”
“全完了——连名誉也完了！”骑士先生说着，一挥手。

“迪安娜！”年轻伯爵绝望地喊。
“先生，您太失身份了。”公爵夫人冷冷地回敬，一面卸下她男人兼情人的双重角色，重新不只是做天使，而且是公爵夫人，而且不只是公爵夫人，还是莫里哀笔下的塞莉梅娜。
德·莫弗里涅斯公爵夫人向这四位人物庄严一躬，从骑士先生那里引出他为女性效劳的最后一滴景仰之泪。
“她多像戈里察公主啊！”他低声感叹。
迪安娜已经消失。驿车夫的鞭响告诉维克图尼安，他初恋情缘的美丽罗曼史结束了。危险未除时，迪安娜在这年轻伯爵身上还看得见她的情人；危险一过，她就鄙视起他的懦弱来。

半年之后，卡缪索获得巴黎助理法官的任命，后来又当上了预审法官。好老头布隆代被任命为王家法院推事；在任上待的日子刚够挣得一份退休金，便回到他那漂亮小宅里去住了。约瑟夫·布隆代坐在父亲的位子上直到寿终；升迁是半点指望也没有了，可他成了布朗德罗小姐的丈夫；而她呢，毫无疑问，如今在那座开满鲜花的砖砌小宅里，过着大理石水盆里鲤鱼一般沉闷的日子。米许和卡缪索还得了荣誉军团十字勋章，布隆代则当了军官。至于

===Original===

Sauvager, deputy public prosecutor, he was sent to Corsica, to du Croisier’s great relief; he had decidedly no mind to bestow his niece upon that functionary.
Du Croisier himself, urged by President du Ronceret, appealed from the finding of the Tribunal to the Court-Royal, and lost his cause. The Liberals throughout the department held that little d’Esgrignon was guilty; while the Royalists, on the other hand, told frightful stories of plots woven by “that abominable du Croisier” to compass his revenge. A duel was fought indeed; the hazard of arms favored du Croisier, the young Count was dangerously wounded, and his antagonist maintained his words. This affair embittered the strife between the two parties; the Liberals brought it forward on all occasions. Meanwhile du Croisier never could carry his election, and saw no hope of marrying his niece to the Count, especially after the duel.
A month after the decision of the Tribunal was confirmed in the Court-Royal, Chesnel died, exhausted by the dreadful strain, which had weakened and shaken him mentally and physically. He died in the hour of victory, like some old faithful hound that has brought the boar to bay, and gets his death on the tusks. He died as happily as might be, seeing that he left the great House all but ruined, and the heir in penury, bored to death by an idle life, and without a hope of establishing himself. That bitter thought and his own exhaustion, no doubt, hastened the old man’s end. One great comfort came to him as he lay amid the wreck of so many hopes, sinking under the burden of so many cares﻿—the old Marquis, at his sister’s entreaty, gave him back all the old friendship. The great lord came to the little house in the Rue du Bercail, and sat by his old servant’s bedside, all unaware how much that servant had done and sacrificed for him. Chesnel sat upright, and repeated Simeon’s cry.﻿—The Marquis allowed them to bury Chesnel in the castle chapel; they laid him crosswise at the foot of the tomb which was waiting for the Marquis himself, the last, in a sense, of the d’Esgrignons.
And so died one of the last representatives of that great and beautiful thing, Service; giving to that often discredited word its original meaning, the relation between feudal lord and servitor.

……索瓦热先生，那位副公诉人，被调到了科西嘉，杜·克鲁瓦西耶如释重负；他是打定主意不肯把内侄女许给这位官员的。
杜·克鲁瓦西耶本人，经院长迪·龙瑟雷撺掇，不服初级法院的裁定上诉到王家法院，败诉了。全郡的自由党人一口咬定小德·埃斯格里尼翁有罪；保王党人那边，则讲着“那个可恶的杜·克鲁瓦西耶”为泄私愤编织阴谋的骇人故事。决斗倒是真打了一场；兵器无眼偏袒了杜·克鲁瓦西耶，年轻伯爵伤势危险，而他的对手寸步不让。这件事使两党的争斗愈演愈烈；自由党人逢场合便把它端出来。此后杜·克鲁瓦西耶的竞选始终不能得手，把内侄女嫁给伯爵更是没了指望，决斗之后尤其如此。
初级法院的裁定在王家法院得到确认之后一个月，谢内尔去世了，被那可怕的紧张拖垮了，身心俱疲，摇摇欲坠。他死在胜利的时刻，像一条忠心的老猎犬，把野猪逼到了绝境，自己死在了獠牙之下。他死得已经算是安适的了，要知道他撇下的是个几乎败落的名门，和一个一贫如洗的继承人——闲得要死，成家的指望全无。这个苦涩的念头，加上他自身的耗竭，无疑加速了老人的死亡。他躺在那么多希望的残骸之中，被那么多操心事压沉下去的时候，得了一个大安慰——老侯爵经妹妹恳求，把旧日的情谊全数还给了他。这位大贵人来到贝卡尔街的小屋，坐在老仆的床边，浑然不知这个仆人为他做过多少、牺牲过多少。谢内尔直起身子，念出了西面的颂歌。——侯爵允许他们把谢内尔葬在城堡的教堂里；他们让他横卧在那座墓的脚下，那座墓是留给侯爵本人的——在某种意义上，他是埃斯格里尼翁家最后一人。
就这样，死去了“效忠”这桩伟大而美好的事物的最后一批代表之一；他给这个常被贬低的词恢复了它最初的意义——封建领主与效忠者之间的关系。

That relation, only to be found in some out-of-the-way province, or among a few old servants of the King, did honor alike to a noblesse that could call forth such affection, and to a bourgeoisie that could conceive it. Such noble and magnificent devotion is no longer possible among us. Noble houses have no servitors left; even as France has no longer a King, nor an hereditary peerage, nor lands that are bound irrevocably to an historic house, that the glorious names of the nation may be perpetuated. Chesnel was not merely one of the obscure great men of private life; he was something more﻿—he was a great fact. In his sustained self-devotion is there not something indefinably solemn and sublime, something that rises above the one beneficent deed, or the heroic height which is reached by a moment’s supreme effort? Chesnel’s virtues belong essentially to the classes which stand between the poverty of the people on the one hand, and the greatness of the aristocracy on the other; for these can combine homely burgher virtues with the heroic ideals of the noble, enlightening both by a solid education.
Victurnien was not well looked upon at Court; there was no more chance of a great match for him, nor a place. His Majesty steadily refused to raise the d’Esgrignons to the peerage, the one royal favor which could rescue Victurnien from his wretched position. It was impossible that he should marry a bourgeoise heiress in his father’s lifetime, so he was bound to live on shabbily under the paternal roof with memories of his two years of splendor in Paris, and the lost love of a great lady to bear him company. He grew moody and depressed, vegetating at home with a careworn aunt and a half heartbroken father, who attributed his son’s condition to a wasting malady. Chesnel was no longer there.
The Marquis died in 1830. The great d’Esgrignon, with a following of all the less infirm noblesse from the Collection of Antiquities, went to wait upon Charles X at Nonancourt; he paid his respects to his sovereign, and swelled the meagre train of the fallen king. It was an act of courage which seems simple enough today, but, in that time of enthusiastic revolt, it was heroism.
“The Gaul has conquered!” These were the Marquis’ last words.
By that time du Croisier’s victory was complete. The new Marquis d’Esgrignon accepted Mlle. Duval as his wife a week after his old father’s death.

那种关系，如今只存在于某个偏远的外省，或国王的几个老仆中间；它同样为两方增光：贵族能唤出这样的深情，资产阶级能生出这样的深情。这样崇高壮丽的忠诚，在我们中间已不可能。名门再没有效忠者了；正如法兰西不再有国王，不再有世袭贵族院，也不再有永远系于一个历史门第的土地，好让民族的光荣姓氏世代相传。谢内尔不只是私生活中那些无名的伟人之一；他更是一种东西——他是一个伟大的事实。在他经久不渝的自我奉献里，难道没有一种难以名状的庄严与崇高，某种超乎一桩善行、超乎一时拼尽全力达到的英雄高度的东西吗？谢内尔的美德，本质上属于那一类阶层：一边是民众的贫贱，一边是贵族的伟大，他们立于两者之间；因为这样的阶层能把市民的朴素德行同贵族的英武理想结合起来，又以扎实的教养使两者生辉。
宫廷里看维克图尼安不顺眼；攀一门好亲、谋一个职位的指望都没有了。陛下坚决不肯把埃斯格里尼翁家升入贵族院——那本是能把维克图尼安从凄凉处境里救出来的唯一恩典。父亲在世之日，他不可能娶一个资产阶级的继承女；于是他只好寒寒酸酸地蛰居在父宅的屋檐下，陪着他的，是巴黎两年繁华的记忆，和一位大夫人逝去了的爱情。他变得郁郁寡欢、委靡不振，在家里的忧心忡忡的姑妈和半心碎的父亲中间草木般地活着；父亲把儿子的状态归咎于一种痨病。谢内尔已经不在了。
侯爵死于1830年。伟大的德·埃斯格里尼翁，身后跟着古物陈列室里腿脚还利落的全体贵族，到农南库尔去觐见查理十世；他向他的君王致敬，给那位倾覆之君冷落的随从队伍添了人数。这在今天看来只是举手之劳，可在那个举国狂热造反的时代，这是英雄行为。
“高卢人得胜了！”这是侯爵的临终遗言。
那时，杜·克鲁瓦西耶的胜利已告完成。老父死后一星期，新任德·埃斯格里尼翁侯爵接受了迪瓦尔小姐做他的妻子。

His bride brought him three millions of francs, for du Croisier and his wife settled the reversion of their fortunes upon her in the marriage-contract. Du Croisier took occasion to say during the ceremony that the d’Esgrignon family was the most honorable of all the ancient houses in France.
Some day the present Marquis d’Esgrignon will have an income of more than a hundred thousand crowns. You may see him in Paris, for he comes to town every winter and leads a jolly bachelor life, while he treats his wife with something more than the indifference of the *grand seigneur* of olden times; he takes no thought whatever for her.
“As for Mlle. d’Esgrignon,” said Émile Blondet, to whom all the detail of the story is due, “if she is no longer like the divinely fair woman whom I saw by glimpses in my childhood, she is decidedly, at the age of sixty-seven, the most pathetic and interesting figure in the Collection of Antiquities. She queens it among them still. I saw her when I made my last journey to my native place in search of the necessary papers for my marriage. When my father knew who it was that I had married, he was struck dumb with amazement; he had not a word to say until I told him that I was a prefect.
“ ‘You were born to it,’ he said, with a smile.
“As I took a walk around the town, I met Mlle. Armande. She looked taller than ever. I looked at her, and thought of Marius among the ruins of Carthage. Had she not outlived her creed, and the beliefs that had been destroyed? She is a sad and silent woman, with nothing of her old beauty left except the eyes, that shine with an unearthly light. I watched her on her way to mass, with her book in her hand, and could not help thinking that she prayed to God to take her out of the world.”

新娘带来300万法郎，因为杜·克鲁瓦西耶夫妇在婚书里把两家财产的继承权都指定给了她。婚礼上，杜·克鲁瓦西耶趁机宣称：德·埃斯格里尼翁家族是法兰西一切古老门第中最有名誉的一家。
有朝一日，当今的德·埃斯格里尼翁侯爵年入将超过10万埃居。你在巴黎可以见到他，他每年冬天进城，过着快活的单身日子，而他对妻子的态度，比旧日大贵人式的冷淡还有过之；他对她根本连想都不想。
“至于德·埃斯格里尼翁小姐，”埃米尔·布隆代说——这段故事的种种细节全亏了他——“如果说她已不复是我童年惊鸿一瞥的那位天仙般的丽人，那么千真万确，她在67岁的年纪，是古物陈列室里最令人感恸、最耐人寻味的人物。她在他们中间依旧母仪俨然。我最后一次回乡办理婚事所需的文书时见过她。我父亲知道我娶的是谁时，惊得哑口无言；直到我告诉他我当了省长，他才说出一句话来。
“‘你天生就是干这个的。’他微笑着说。
“我在城里散步时，遇见了阿尔芒德小姐。她看上去比从前更高了。我望着她，想起迦太基废墟上的马略。她不也正是活过了她的信条、活过了那些已被摧毁的信仰吗？她是一个忧伤而沉默的女人，旧日的美貌一样不剩，只有那双眼睛还在，闪着非人间的光。我看着她手里拿着祈祷书去望弥撒，禁不住想：她是在求上帝把她从这个人世带走。”
